## What is Jev

Jev is TypeSafe AI's first **System One model**. It does not write prose. It takes a state plus questions whose answer space you define in advance, and returns typed values with probability distributions your code can branch on.

|                  |                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Shape**        | `state + typed questions` → `constrained answers + probabilities` → `your code`                                                                                                                          |
| **Primitives**   | `Choice` (pick one of ≤255 options), `Score` (a rubric 2–10), `Noul` (a probabilistic yes/no)                                                                                                            |
| **Endpoint**     | `POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`, model `jev-1.13.0` / alias `jev-latest`                                                                                                                     |
| **Good fits**    | routing, triage, scoring, moderation, verification and low-latency gates inside a bounded workflow                                                                                                       |
| **Known limits** | counting is unreliable, multi-level indirection is weak, and official material names nine classes of jaggedness. Schema-valid output is not the same as a correct decision — calibrate on your own data. |

## How entries are graded

Most lists in this space assert inclusion. This one says how much it actually verified, then lets you filter accordingly.

| Grade        | What it means                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `official`   | Published by TypeSafe AI itself.                                                                                                                          |
| `observed`   | A public artifact that can be opened and read — real source, real configuration, or an explicit TypeSafe/Jev declaration in the repository name or topic. |
| `inferred`   | Matched on an ambiguous signal plus corroborating vocabulary, but not yet read line by line.                                                              |
| `unverified` | Looks related, nothing independently confirmed. Listed for discovery only.                                                                                |

<a id="agent-tooling"></a>

## Agent tooling: MCP, hooks, gates and coding agents

The fastest-growing category: hooks, MCP servers and gates that put a typed decision in front of an agent's next action.

<details>
<summary>🤖 <b><a href="https://github.com/tamaratran/fast-jev-compaction">tamaratran/fast-jev-compaction</a></b> · ⭐7244 · TypeScript · 🔎 inferred · 12d · ⭐+5</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

Claude Code plugin that replaces the compaction summary with Jev decisions: every tool call and result is scored in one fast request, stale ones are dropped or truncated, everything kept stays verbatim.

> 💡 Replaces a coding agent's context-compaction summary with a typed decision. A clean example of swapping one LLM call in an existing pipeline rather than rebuilding the pipeline.

<a id="routing-guardrails"></a>

## Routing, guardrails and approvals

The production-shaped use case — send each request to the cheapest model that can actually handle it, and keep a deterministic check on the outcome.

<details>
<summary>🛡️ <b><a href="https://github.com/NandhaKishorM/laya">NandhaKishorM/laya</a></b> · ⭐29164 · Python · 👁️ observed · 1d · ⭐+68</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

Non-autoregressive System 1 decision engine. Typed choice, score and yes/no decisions over any text in a single forward pass, in 100+ languages, with a router that picks the right checkpoint per request.

<sub>🔧 Found used in code: `research/benchmarks/feishu_zh/run.py`</sub>

<a id="evaluation"></a>

## Evaluation, calibration and benchmarks

How anyone knows the decisions are any good. Calibration is the open question in this ecosystem, and these are the projects measuring it.

##### 📝 Summary

mini-Jev: what a Jev-style typed-decision interface looks like on a frozen Qwen3-4B — read the option letter's logits instead of generating JSON. Preregistered experiment, results, teaching bench.

> 💡 The most useful independent reproduction to read: it shows the read-the-logits mechanism working, and it also warns explicitly that the share it reads out is not a calibrated probability. That warning is the single most important caveat in this ecosystem.

<details>
<summary>🎮 <b><a href="https://x.com/tspy/status/2100864234523685146">Xtags — X intent labeller</a></b> · @tspy · 👁️ observed · 12d</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

Xtags: a Chrome extension that labels each post in an X timeline with what it is trying to get from you — inducement, provocation, promotion, machine-generated, persuasion, entertainment, information — drawn as a tag plus a probability right after the timestamp. A side panel reports session counts (seen, judged, correct) and cumulative token cost. The author reports near-instant responses and usable accuracy before any tuning.

➡️ **Original project (manifoldor/xtags)** — [https://github.com/manifoldor/xtags](https://github.com/manifoldor/xtags)

> 💡 Worth reading as a latency argument rather than an accuracy one: labelling a timeline only works if the decision costs less than the scroll, which is the constraint a generative model cannot meet.

<a id="media-discussions"></a>

## Writing, discussions and sibling lists

Launch threads, independent write-ups and the other curated lists in this space. This repository is not the only one, and saying so is more useful than pretending otherwise.

<details>
<summary>📰 <b><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/model-jaggedness/jev-1.13">9 类已知缺陷（jaggedness）</a></b> · ✅ official · 13d</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

Literal understanding, **unreliable counting**, date comparison, multiple levels of indirection, large state with irrelevant details, adversarial content, contradictions between instructions and criteria, structural invariants, and generation. The official recommendation is to "split them into atomic problems and combine them in code".

> 💡 **Capability boundaries**—the vendor's own list of boundaries, fully archived

##### 📌 Basic facts

| Field    | Value                                    |
| -------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| Category | `Writing, discussions and sibling lists` |
| Evidence | `official`                               |

##### 📊 Data

| Metric       | Value      |
| ------------ | ---------- |
| First listed | 2026-09-20 |

🏷 `collection` · `官方`

</details>

<details>
<summary>📰 <b><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/cookbooks/consistency_choice_cookbook">`consistency_choice` cookbook 正式回答了&quot;重复调用结果会变&quot;</a></b> · ✅ official · 13d</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

The same review rubric, **8 Choice × each condition repeated 15 times**. TypeSafe **raw agree 90.8%** (2 of 8 questions flipped, but **conflicts=0**—the flips occurred only between "specific label ↔ uncertain"). **After adding an abstention rule (using `probabilities` to require top probability ≥ 0.60; otherwise `uncertain` routes to a human), agreement rose to 99.2%**, automatic 74.2%, **with no increase in model calls**. Comparison: Haiku t=0 100%, Opus 4.8 reasoning 92.5%, gpt-5.4-mini t=0 99.2%. Official statement: this does not make the model certain; these figures measure **repeatability, not correctness**; 0.60 is an example strategy, and production thresholds should be determined using labeled samples and costs.

> 💡 **\[DEFINITION-CHANGE\]** Updated B5, the key figures table, SKILL.md hard rule 3 and launch item 6, and applicability boundary item 6—**this library previously recorded "non-determinism" as a risk; it is now changed to the official correct approach: manage it with abstention**

<details>
<summary>📰 <b><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/confidence">confidence 的官方语义确认</a></b> · ✅ official · 13d</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

The official documentation explicitly states that `confidence` is a **statistic calculated from the `probabilities` distribution**; the more concentrated the distribution, the more certain it is; **Noul does not carry confidence**; it explicitly says "you are not locked into our definition"; thresholds should "start from a conservative value and measure on your own data."

> 💡 Consistent with Archer's inverse formula, written into the definition file

<details>
<summary>📰 <b><a href="https://www.langchain.com/blog/building-a-harness-with-jev（2026-09-17">LangChain 官方博文解释了&quot;为什么 agent 护栏品类爆发&quot; — ，Sydney Runkle &amp; Hunter Lovell）</a></b> · ✅ official</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

*"Coding harnesses like claude, codex, cursor have shipped some kind of way to classify dangerous actions… **Up until now, this classifier step has been locked away in the closed source parts of the harness. Now that a cheap and performant classifier model exists, we can take the same pattern and adopt it to all agents!**"*; it also provides a router example (using Jev to select a model according to custom criteria, with probabilities retained in agent state) and *"Jev isn't a drop-in replacement for an LLM"*.

> 💡 **Explains the "productization of guardrail supplies" observed by this library**—open-sourced because of closed-source harness features; written into snippet library §2.5

<details>
<summary>📰 <b><a href="https://docs.typesafe.ai/models">官方文档索引与限流/上下文/定制政策全部核实</a></b> · ✅ official · 13d</summary>

##### 📝 Summary

Endpoint `POST https://api.typesafe.ai/v1/systemone`; `jev-1.13.0`, aliases `jev-latest`/`jev-preview`; 64k/request, state + maximum question length 32k; rate limits of 250k tok/s and 1200 req/min with **dynamic adjustment**; text input only; Choice≤255, Score 2–10 tiers; **no fine-tuning, all accounts share the same weights**; customer data is not used for training; most accurate in English.

> 💡 **Architecture/capability boundaries**—written into `01-系统定义/jev-系统定义.md` and `jev-能力与边界-准确定义.md`

##### 📝 Summary

Preview endpoint `/preview/evaluation` → stable version **`POST /v1/systemone`**; `prompts` array → `questions` map; `responses` array → `answers` map; fields renamed **`probability`→`noul`, `chosen`→`choice`, `expectation`→`score`**; choice probabilities changed from an array to a map; **score probabilities are newly added in v1**; authentication unchanged.

> 💡 Added to the archive `官方文档快照/migrating-to-v1.md`; this also provides a **second official-page discovery channel** (the documentation routing table for the official skill)

## How this list stays current

No human edits the body of this README. The repository runs a five-stage pipeline on a schedule and only commits when something actually changed.

<img src="assets/readme/pipeline.svg" width="100%" alt="How this list stays current">

|             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **collect** | GitHub search across a query matrix, the official organisation, GitHub code search, Hacker News and the Hugging Face hub.                                                                                                                                           |
| **curate**  | deterministic and LLM-free, so two consecutive runs on the same input produce byte-identical output. A two-signal rule decides relevance; name collisions (JeVois, JEvents, Jevil, jEveAssets, ESP32-RLCD and similar) are excluded by an explicit, auditable list. |
| **media**   | collects each project's own screenshots and screen recordings. Assets are only copied into this repository when the project declares a redistribution-friendly licence; otherwise the upstream URL is hot-linked and the card says so.                              |
| **render**  | produces every language edition from one template, so the twenty READMEs can never drift apart in structure.                                                                                                                                                        |
| **audit**   | fails the build if an entry lacks a URL, if a link is dead, if two entries duplicate a URL, or if a README drifts from its generated form.                                                                                                                          |

## Contributing

Corrections are welcome and are the fastest way to improve this list. Open an issue or a pull request if an entry is misfiled, mis-graded, or if a project has been wrongly excluded as a name collision — that last category is where automated filters are most likely to be wrong. Additions are best made by adding a source to `scripts/collect.py` rather than by editing the README, because the README is regenerated on every tick.

---

<sub>Independent community project. Not affiliated with, endorsed by, or reviewed by TypeSafe AI. Product behaviour, pricing, limits and model aliases change without notice; verify anything load-bearing against the official documentation. Assets remain the property of their upstream projects and are reproduced only where a licence permits.</sub>
